ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U391	Seventh item: Second reading of the draft Act amending § 7 of the National Defence Act initiated by the National Defence Committee. For the presentation, I invite Madis Milling, Member of the National Defence Commission. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U392	Honourable Vice-Chairman of the Riigikogu! Ladies and gentlemen, The National Defence Committee initiated a draft s. a on 20 April, by consensus. The first reading of the draft was completed on 10 May. The deadline for submitting amendments, which was 15 May at 10.30 a.m., did not propose any amendments to the draft. The National Defence Committee discussed the draft before submitting it to second reading at its meeting on 15 May, where it examined the opinion of the Government of the Republic on the draft. The Government of the Republic discussed the draft at its meeting on 3 May and decided to support it in principle. The Government of the Republic also recommended in the draft the use of the general term 'Strategic Development Document on National Defence' and the waiver of the requirement that the Strategic Development Document on National Defence contains state secrets. Indeed, § 7 (1) of the National Defence Act refers to strategic development documents for national defence, but this provision indicates that they are drafted, implemented and amended on the basis of the procedures for drawing up, implementing and amending strategic development documents laid down in the State Budget Act and the legislation given on the basis thereof. According to § 19 of the State Budget Act, strategic development documents are the fundamentals of politics, the development plan of the field, the development plan of the government area and the programme. Pursuant to § 20 (2) of the State Budget Act, the development plan in the field of national defence will also be submitted to the Riigikogu for discussion before being approved by the Government of the Republic. The draft law 438 sets out the specificity of the process of handling the development plan in the field of national defence, which contains only state secrets and which is exposed to NATO classified external information. The aim of the National Defence Committee is not to extend the draft and to impose an obligation on the Prime Minister to provide the National Defence Committee with other national defence development documents for its opinion, which is the development plan and programme of the government area approved by the Minister. The relevant national defence strategic development documents, not all of which contain state secrets, could be consulted by the National Defence Committee in the framework of parliamentary scrutiny or at the initiative of the Minister for Foreign Affairs. The latter act should not be amended to the law. Procedure: At its meeting on 15 May, the National Defence Committee approved by consensus the text of the second reading and the explanatory memorandum and decided by consensus to propose to the Riigikogu to stop the second reading. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U394	The next item is the first reading of Draft Act 459 on Prevention of Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing, initiated by the Government of the Republic. I'd like you to present a speech chair to Secretary of Finance, Sven Sester.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U395	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Dear Members, I will give an overview of the draft law on the prevention of money laundering and terrorist financing, which is number 459. We are taking over the fourth anti-money laundering directive, which was adopted in May 2015. The new directive was developed because the Financial Action Task Force, an international organisation setting standards for the prevention of money laundering and terrorist financing, introduced new rules in 2012. Estonia has so far been internationally active and, according to estimates, a good anti-money laundering agent. However, we have not escaped the interest of criminals to move black money this way either. In 2016, the Supreme Court was finally convicted of Estonia's first terrorist funders, who supported ISIS with their actions. The issue is therefore topical and we have an international obligation to do our utmost to prevent these activities. The draft provides for a number of instruments for this purpose. First, the obligation to apply so-called due diligence measures and the principle of 'know your client' remain in force. In the new law, the application of due diligence measures has been made more flexible in order to allow for better account to be taken of the different business profiles and the potential risks they pose. Another important change is the introduction of a risk-based approach to a new level. The risk will be assessed proactively, the risk analysis must be drawn up by both the State and the other obliged entities referred to in that law. The aim is greater flexibility and attention to where it is most needed. Each undertaking can best understand its own risks; the national risk assessment provides the right direction and references to which attention should be paid, if necessary. The third and most relevant change is the institution of the real beneficiaries and, more generally, the increase in transparency in the ownership structures of legal entities. International scandals such as Panamanian paperwork and a more coordinated fight against tax evasion have raised the need to make information about the real beneficiaries more accessible, both at European and G20 level and at OECD level. For this purpose, a system of queries concerning the actual beneficiaries will be established at the Estonian business register. This information will be public, otherwise the beneficiaries associated with legal entities with complex ownership structures would be in the preferred situation compared to the ordinary owners for whom the information is publicly available. The technical solution is ready on 1 December 2017 and by the beginning of February all legal entities should publish their actual beneficiaries in the commercial register. The registrar shall send out notification notes and reminders so that everyone is informed of this requirement in good time. I know that it is also interesting what happens to entrepreneurs who do not fulfil this obligation. The aim of the State is not to rush to impose or fine penalties on this issue. This is a global initiative with a clear objective, but the journey has only just been marked off the map. For example, in a few years' time, the European Commission intends to connect the registers of the beneficial owners through a common platform so that a request from one Member State will also indicate the beneficiary of a legal person in the register of another Member State. For this reason, we have foreseen in the draft that the penalty for a specific infringement should not enter into force until 2019. Information on the actual beneficiaries will make the business environment more transparent. Only subjects are legally obliged to check the actual beneficiaries, but the records of the actual beneficiaries are likely to be used in other areas where background checks are carried out. This means, above all, changing practice, but the Ministry of Finance will finally carry out an analysis of whether it is necessary to amend legislation in this regard. As a last important change, I would like to emphasise the deepening of international cooperation between supervisors and FIUs, the necessity of which has been highlighted, as has the ability to respond quickly, particularly due to terrorist attacks in recent times in the capitals of the European Union. Estonian laws have been criticised from this point of view because the penalty rates in our laws are low compared to those in other countries. The draft law raises the threshold for criminal penalties for legal persons up to EUR 400000. Like other financial sector legislation, enforcement rates are increased. In order to enforce the same obligation, a maximum of EUR 5 million may be imposed; in the case of a legal person, it may amount to up to 10% of the total annual turnover of the legal person. The law enters into force in general, but obliged persons have one year to comply with the new requirements. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U396	You have questions too. Krista Aru, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U397	Thank you, Mr President. ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U398	Thank you. This issue has been discussed before. The sanction rates for misdemeanours laid down in the Act must be established in accordance with the general part of the Penal Code and the general part of the Code. Since 2015, the Penal Code provides for a financial penalty of EUR 100000 to EUR 400000 for misdemeanour; we have now put it to the maximum. If we want to change this in the future, I know that at the end of last year, the Ministry of Justice sent out a plan of development aimed primarily at raising the fines for misdemeanour in the financial sector, it is a separate issue. We've already reached the maximum threshold.

ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U401	Thank you, that's a good question! We really have. As you said, it is precisely so that the more flexible you go with regard to due diligence, the more opportunities for abuse become. They've really been trying to find that balance. Today, we can say that our new technological opportunities give us the necessary flexibility. If, for example, personal identification was very rigid in the past, the person now does not have to come on site because of technological possibilities. All parties are always in the business. It serves this purpose, on the one hand, to keep the risks under control and, on the other, to give our own fintech companies the opportunity to develop.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U402	Aivar Sõerd.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U403	Thank you, Mr President. Dear Minister, Compared to the other countries of the European Union, we still have very gentle penalties for misdemeanour provided for in this draft. ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U404	When I answered one of the previous questions, I touched on this subject to some extent, but then I will repeat a few thoughts. But, as I mentioned, in the picture of the financial sector as a whole, it is planned to revise the fine rates in the future. At the end of last year, the Ministry of Justice sent out an intention to develop it. If this intention to develop becomes a real law at some point, we will have a window of opportunity.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U405	Thank you, Minister. No further questions for you right now. The Chair of the Monetary Commission, Mikhail Stalnukhin, will make a presentation on behalf of the Steering Committee.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U406	Mr President! Ladies and gentlemen, This draft law was discussed by the Committee on Monetary Affairs on 5 May inclusive. The Government of the Republic was represented by the Minister of Finance, Mr Sester. He introduced the substance of the bill exactly as he just did here. After that, there was a brief debate. Three questions were asked. Firstly, there was interest in the extent to which the new law reflects the Euro Directive and how much our own creations are there. The answer was that it is largely based on the Euro Directive, but also on our specific features. Indeed, if the new law enters into force, the law will apply to non-profit organisations if they are paid or paid in cash themselves more than EUR 5000. In the current law, this amount is EUR 15000. The FIU shall have the right to suspend the transaction or to restrict the disposal of the asset, where appropriate. Initially, the limit is set for up to 30 days, but the office has the right to extend it by 60 days, or 90 days in total. In particularly serious cases, the procedure may be extended for up to one year, but not more. The Directive establishes a register of actual beneficiaries. This is information on the beneficial owners of companies published in the Commercial Register Information System. Estonia makes it to the Registries and Information Systems Centre, because the business register is already a functioning information system, where an additional service should be created. The second question was whether this law will guarantee us more effective and active international cooperation. The single answer was: yes, that's right. The question was also about the penalty rate: the financial penalty imposed on a legal person will be EUR 400000 instead of EUR 32000. They asked whether this was a European standard, and the answer was that this was the maximum that our legal space would allow. The Commission adopted procedural decisions, all by consensus. The chairman of the committee was appointed rapporteur. It was decided to propose to the Riigikogu Board that the draft hall be brought to an end on 17 May and that the first reading be completed and that the deadline for amendments be 31 May at 5.15 p.m. I would also like to add that, although we have not yet completed the first reading, the Commission has already received a letter from an interested organisation, which raises some questions about the penalty rates and the length of these periods. I think there are quite interesting questions. We have something to discuss between the first reading and the second reading. This is AS TavexWise, who sent this letter, and we will forward it to the Ministry of Finance. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U414	The next item is the first reading of the draft Act 454 on simplified taxation of business income initiated by the Government of the Republic. To present the speech chair, Minister of Finance Sven Sester!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U415	Thank you, Mr President. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I give an overview of the draft law on simplified taxation of business income, which bears number 454. In the future, the easiest way to start and do business is to open an entrepreneurial account. That's what you could call it in Estonian. In order to facilitate micro-business, the draft aims to introduce a special tax regime under which tax liability is to be met in a simplified and low uniform rate of taxation. This special scheme is suitable for anyone who sells goods or provides services up to EUR 25000 per year. The taxpayer must open an entrepreneur's account at the bank that receives its income. Of the amounts received, the bank will automatically book 20% to cover the tax liability and pay it to the tax authorities in the following month. Thus, 80 cents will remain in the hands of each of the euro-earned entrepreneurs. Payment of business income tax will be subject to the payment of income tax, social tax and compulsory funded pension. A small enterprise using an entrepreneur account does not need to submit any reports or spend time on accounting. Therefore, operating through an entrepreneur account has been made as convenient as possible. The payment of business income tax also provides the recipient with social guarantees. This ensures, if there is sufficient income, health insurance for the period of operation and future pension, and increases the cash benefits of the company in the form of parental benefit or temporary incapacity benefit. The amount of benefits depends on the amount of the social security contribution paid. Medical insurance is required to receive at least 1300 euros per month in an entrepreneur's account. Bureaucracy has been minimised in the case of an entrepreneurial account. The taxpayer shall not be obliged to keep accounts or to submit tax returns or other accounts. Everything is automatically done through an entrepreneur account, which automatically collects the tax. If the draft is approved, business accounts can be opened as early as 1 January 2018. The creation of a simplified way of dealing with this kind of business offers opportunities to earn additional income from business to those for whom self-employed or company-based activities have seemed too complex or impractical due to the small size of the business. For those who currently provide services on a small scale, an entrepreneurial account will provide an easier opportunity to be an honest taxpayer. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U416	You have questions too. Raivo Põldaru, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U417	Thank you, Mr President-in-Office. Minister, thank you very much. We take the theoretical option that the entrepreneur recommends that his employees quit their jobs and start operating through the same business account. Why don't you give us a little enlightenment?
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U418	We have also made sure that the same thing that happened in Latvia does not happen. What happened in Latvia was that they wanted to develop entrepreneurship, but the optimization of taxes started to take place, which you referred to in your question. When asked whether it was possible to provide a service to a legal person, it was assumed at the very beginning that this possibility could not arise. This is precisely in view of the example of Latvia. We also discussed with various tax consultants, including Lasse Lehise, the head of the Estonian Taxpayers' Association, who paid attention to this. He said it would be reasonable to give them this opportunity and suggested a possible solution, which was also mentioned here. Thus, in the future it will also be possible to provide a service to a legal person and to prevent employees from becoming entrepreneurs if they are not in fact undertakings, the legal persons and self-employed persons will be obliged to pay income tax on the fee paid. This is Paragraph 51 (2) (1) of the Income Tax Act. As a result, they will have to pay an additional 20%. The recipient pays the holder of the business account but pays an additional 20% and as a result the tax burden for the labour force and the business account is completely the same and there is no incentive to direct its employees to provide the service through the business account. That's what we're gonna do nicely to solve this problem.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U419	Please, Maris Lauri!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U420	Thank you. Good minister! I want to ask you about the rates and the amount that has been put into the bill. Where does EUR 25000 come from? Why is it $25,000, not $40,000 or some other amount?
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U421	Thank you. I think that's a very good question. There were also several discussions on this subject. The question is who and to what extent we want to involve. EUR 25000 is just over EUR 2000 per month. It would be a classic micro-business, not even a small business service. And after the discussions, it remained EUR 25000. I don't think it would be right for this money to get a lot bigger at some point. As you know, the VAT obligation already arises at EUR 40000, so you have to sell your service already at VAT. There were several discussions on this subject. There was also the view that perhaps this border should be brought down, just to take down the risks that the previous speaker had in mind when he spoke about providing services to legal persons. However, in summary, it was found that about EUR 2 000 per month could be the amount for which we can say that holding an entrepreneur account would be reasonable, practical and constructive. The funds will also be received on the business account after the full amount of 25000 euros, but then not 20% will be withheld from the entrepreneur account holder, but 40% will be withheld. He loses the idea of transferring to that account or paying more than EUR 25000 in cash.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U422	Andres Ammas.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U423	Respectable waitress! Honourable President! You referred to Lasse Lehise from the Estonian Taxpayers' Association. A letter from the taxpayer's association has also been received in which it will look more closely at all the proposed tax changes and, however, criticise this new draft. It points out that there is no measure to prevent public authorities, local authorities and legal persons from formalising a service contract with a natural person. He gives quite a graphic and distinctive example. I quote: 'For example, a member of the Riigikogu may hire a domestic servant, a driver or an assistant and pay a fee for the cost benefit, which would ultimately be taxed at 20% tax only, then register in the register of employees of the service provider and pay all labour taxes. ' Please, would you comment on that?
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U424	The draft has not been drawn up on the basis of who will pay and for what and which service will be provided. Our aim has been to create a situation where it is possible to offer or sell our services and to do so as conveniently as possible, with as little red tape as possible and with the lowest possible tax rate. I think that is precisely what this particular draft is for.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U425	Andres Herkel.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U426	Thank you, Mr President. Dear Minister, I hope, however, that this is not the way to use the cost compensation. Maris Lauri has already asked where this EUR 25000 limit comes from, could this amount not be higher. Here's what I'm asking. In your presentation, you said that health insurance is conditional on the receipt of at least 1300 euros per month on an entrepreneur's account. Why $1,300? And is that some kind of average, or is that the minimum every month? What happens if 1,100 euros are received in one month and 1,500 euros in the other month?
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U427	Thank you, that is a very good question, and it is certainly worth talking about. In Estonia, health insurance is based on the principle of solidarity, and this EUR 1300 comes from it. It should be taken into account that no person should have the advantage of receiving health insurance on the basis of the income they receive. We have to take into account that the holder of an entrepreneur account has a reduced tax rate: each of its euro is taxed only at 20%, and that 20% is divided into three: income tax, social tax and savings pension. It is a person who is not engaged in anything else, he does not have any basic work, so that this micro-business is like a side job, and because he is subject to a reduced tax rate, we must be guided by the fact that insurance cover is always based on solidarity. A certain calculation has shown that, in the case of EUR 1300, it contributes jointly and severally to the health insurance fund with regard to its social tax. The second part of the question was whether the sum would sum up and pass from one month to the next. Nothing changes in that sense. Just as it is now, it will be in the future. The income required to receive health insurance is calculated on a calendar basis. It is the same now, and it will continue in the future, with no change coming.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U428	Krista Aru, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U429	Thank you, Mr President. Dear Minister, The analysis of the impact of the explanatory memorandum shows nicely the possible behaviour of self-employed workers, as well as the potential damage they may suffer. However, the other half of the information work has been left unanswered: who informs the self-employed that opening this account might be rather detrimental to them because of the possible reduction of these social protection benefits?
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U430	Thank you. If you look at the impact analysis, you will see that self-employed workers do not suffer, but rather benefit. The damage actually happens to the state budget. As strange as it may seem, as a result of this law, we expect 1 million minus for the state budget in the first stage. Not a plus! In principle, on the one hand, there is, of course, a picture of people who currently do not declare their daily activities at all. This addition will certainly have a positive impact on the state budget. But on the other hand, what you just asked about will also work. Self-employed, self-employed, are pragmatic people; they calculate their daily costs well. And it is good for them to open an entrepreneur's account that guarantees them a reduced tax rate of 20% and that they do not have to squabble with bureaucracy at all, and no one will have to report any reports to either the business register, the tax office, Statistics Estonia or Eesti Pank. But on the other hand, they can't record their expenses in the report. This is certainly also an important factor. So, of course, the self-employed person will see which one is more useful to him. If it has a very cost-intensive production, it is most likely that it will not be suitable for the completion of the business account. The business account logic is that it gives one additional value to the option. Now this issue of informing the parties. ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U449	The tenth item is the first reading of the Bill 460 on Amendment to the Packaging Excise Act, the Packaging Act and the Environmental Charges Act, initiated by the Government of the Republic. Minister Sven Sester, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U450	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Dear Members, I present to you the draft law amending the Package Tax Act, the Packaging Act and the Environmental Charges Act, which bears the number 460. The amendment to the Packaging Excise Act is due to a number of factors, the most important of which is that packages are placed on the market in very large quantities in Estonia and that data relating to packaging are unreliable. In Estonia, 170 kg of packaging per capita is consumed in the middle of the year. In this respect, we are sixth in Europe, which means that we consume significantly more than we would expect in comparison with our purchasing power and other consumption figures. The quantities of packaging are on an annual growth trend, although the population is decreasing. The current Packaging Excise Act does not affect the loss of packaging mass consumed in Estonia, but regulates the obligation to re-use packaging. The law is not about reasons, it is about consequences. In addition, there are shortcomings in the current regulation as regards the reporting of the placing on the market of packaging and the correct recording of packaging waste. The National Audit Office has also referred to the problem in 2010 in the report 'Performance of collection and recovery of packaging waste'. Due to these problems, the amendment of the Packaging Excise Act was already introduced into the Programme of Action of the Government of the Republic in 2015. The legal changes are based on the study of the University of Tartu's Centre for Social Science Applied Research and the Stockholm Environmental Institute Tallinn Centre'Package taxation analysis based on the assessment of the life cycle of packaging'. The study highlights the shortcomings of the current system and makes recommendations for changes to the system. All of this has been largely taken into account in the draft. The bill amends the procedure for the taxation of excise duties on packaging as follows: The current system for regulating the re-use of packaging remains dependent on the collection and recovery of packaging waste. Exemptions from excise duty shall also remain in force if the packages have been re-used within the prescribed targets. The rates of excise duty are based on the indicative rates indicated in the study, which are linked to the environmental burden of packaging. The new rates are designed to reduce the excise duty of 75% on wood and 33% on paper. The metal excise duty does not change, while the rates of 20% plastic and 33% glass packaging increase. I will also mention the new rates of excise duty if the targets for the recycling of packaging are not reached: EUR 3 per kilogram of plastic, EUR 2,5 per kilogram of metal, EUR 0.8 per kilogram of glass, EUR 0.8 per kilogram of paper and cardboard, EUR 0.8 per kilogram of wood and EUR 0.3 per kilogram of wood. Unlike the current law, the draft also regulates the placing on the market of packaging. This is aimed at reducing packaging quantities and using packages of more environmentally friendly materials. According to the draft, goods packages placed on the market in Estonia and acquired and imported from another Member State are taxed on their placing on the market. The rates of excise duty for the placing on the market of packaging are ten times lower than the rates of non-compliance with the targets for the re-use of packaging. However, the excise duty on the placing on the market of packaging is not linked to compliance with the obligation to re-use packaging and therefore the tax cannot be avoided by applying the recovery of packaging waste. Ecolabelled products and their packaging shall be exempt from excise duty applicable to placing on the market. Re-use packages shall be taxed for the first time on their placing on the market. The excise duty paid for the transport packaging shall be refunded if the packaging is removed from Estonia during the year. The draft also amends the procedure for providing information on the placing of packaging on the market. All packaging companies will start to submit data on packaging placed on the market to the packaging register managed by the Environmental Agency on a quarterly basis. Both the Tax and Customs Board and recycling organisations receive data from there. The requirements for reporting re-use and reporting to the packaging register are unchanged. In order to facilitate the declaration, the exchange of data between the packaging register and the e-Tax Board will take place via the X-Road. There are possibilities for the company not to submit a separate declaration of excise duties on packaging to the Tax and Customs Board, but for the tax authorities to receive information from the Environmental Agency's packaging register, which establishes a pre-filled excise declaration. It will be easier to pay taxes. The law will enter into force on 1 January 2018, if Parliament supports it, and will receive an amount of EUR 15 million. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U604	Honourable President! Respectable Minister of Finance! Respectable members of the Riigikogu! Do you know that today it has become clear that the excise duty on packaging already exists? Under the current system, packaging operators are obliged to keep records of packages placed on the market by quantity and type. They are obliged to re-use the packaging and, if the undertaking itself is not able to do so, they will have to pay excise duty in accordance with the current rules. And since most packaging companies are not able to recover the packaging of the goods they sell from the market, or because the cost of such activities would simply be unreasonable for them, they have the opportunity, but in essence the obligation to join a recycling organisation. In this respect, it is noteworthy that this country itself monitors the accuracy of the declared quantities of packaging, in fact the State has given the packaging company the obligation to audit this packaging at its own expense. Now to the draft to be dealt with. In summary, the Ministry of Finance points out, and I quote: 'In addition, there are shortcomings in the current regulation relating to the correct recording and reporting of packaging and packaging waste and partially ineffective monitoring due to poor reporting. ' When you talk to the packaging companies, they say the Ministry is absolutely right. This objective of reducing packaging and packaging in circulation has not been achieved so far and is due to shortcomings in previous regulation and ineffective monitoring. But instead of correcting errors, making the system more effective in any way, the faulty system, as it is, is now left with the next obligation on the packaging company in the form of additional excise duties on packaging. During the meeting of the Monetary Commission, Marek Uusküla had a lot of talk about this possible supervision. However, despite all the explanations, it seems to the speaker here that it is very blue-eyed to hope that the new regime will now be so effective as to ensure that the objective of environmental impact is achieved. And it is also blue-eyed to hope that an unequal competitive environment with this new additional burden will somehow improve. Where does this inequality come from? This is due to unfair packaging companies that do not declare them. There are, and not few, these businesses, which have been shown by the business organisations themselves. More time, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U605	Please, three more minutes!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U606	So the gap between honest and unfair operators is likely to increase; the competitive advantage of an unfair undertaking may still increase. For businesses, it is unclear that the draft under consideration provides that the same data must now be submitted in parallel to the packaging register. Consequently, the data to date is not reliable. This means that the state does not consider this current audited packaging accounting to be reliable. Why, then, do packaging companies need to spend a considerable amount of money each year to audit packaging data? The objectives of the law, as has been mentioned today, are repeatedly mentioned in terms of environmental impact and the desire to motivate packaging companies to reduce the quantities of packaging placed on the market, to use more environmentally friendly packaging materials and to guide consumers to use packaging more sustainably. ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U618	Now begins the first reading of the Bill 457 of the Law on Sweet Drink Tax initiated by the Government of the Republic. The initiating waitress, Secretary of Finance Sven Sester, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U619	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! Glad to see that... so many members are in the hall. [[The manager's ringing the bell.]] You've got the bill for the sweet drink tax bill before you. Health studies, including those of the Ministry of Social Affairs, R&D&I, as well as those of the European WHO, were being developed. The conclusions drawn from these studies are worrying and require attention. In Estonia, obesity is one of the highest in comparison with other EU Member States. This is accompanied by the rapid spread of diseases associated with over-consumption of sugar.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U620	Honourable Riigikogu and MP Sults, please do not disturb the waitress!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U621	The role is also played by the fact that processed energy-rich food is cheap and available in terms of energy content and price. This also applies to sugar-sweetened drinks. The health protection objective pursued by the introduction of this tax involves directing economic operators towards the production of beverages with a lower sugar and sweetener content and influencing the consumer to make sustainable choices. The introduction of a sweet drink tax is both a protection of health and a fiscal objective. The liver brings about a change in the price of sweetened drinks, affecting consumers and producers at the same time. It also raises public awareness of the health effects of sugar. The tax rates provided for in the draft act depend on sugar content in order to encourage producers to use less sugar in their products and to influence consumer health behaviour in a positive way. With a limit of EUR 0,1 per litre of drink, the sweetener and sugar content shall be subject to a charge of between 5 and 8 grams per 100 millilitres. Where a drink contains more than 8 grams of sugar per 100 ml of drink, it shall be taxed at EUR 0,3 per litre. Where a beverage contains both sweetener and more than 5 grams of sugar per 100 ml, it shall be taxed on the sugar content. The draft does not necessarily support the switch from sugar to sweetener when the drinks are sweetened. The sweet matter content is always taxed, whereas in the case of a beverage sweetened with sugar, the levy may be released at all if the sugar is less than 5 grams per 100 ml. The tax treatment of sweet matter also takes into account the fact that sweeteners have a daily safe intake and that their health effects have not been sufficiently studied. In response to the wishes of the producers, a two-year transitional period has been introduced for the taxation of drinks with a higher sugar content. During 2018, those drinks containing 10 or more grams of sugar per 100 ml and those containing 9 or more grams of sugar shall be taxed at a higher rate during 2019. From 2020 onwards, the final rate to be charged at a higher rate of 8 grams of sugar per 100 ml of drink will apply. The aim of the transitional period is to give producers time to change recipes and consumers the opportunity to get used to new flavors. Fruit and vegetables to which no sugar or sweeteners have been added shall be subject to an authorisation to grant State aid and shall not be subject to any tax as from the date of authorisation. The application is being prepared and submitted by the Ministry of Finance to the European Commission in May. The objective of the exemption is to ensure that healthy alternatives are still available to the consumer. The juice has largely preserved the nutritional value of the raw materials and has a high biovalue. Now look at the exceptions contained in the draft. Sweetened beverages used in production in the course of an economic or professional activity shall not be subject to taxation. Understandably, these drinks do not become available to the consumer in their original form or fall within the scope of the law as a raw material for production. Thus, sweetened beverages used in the production of other sweetened beverages, food, medicines and alcohol are not taxed. Sweetened drinks brought to Estonia for the personal consumption of travellers shall not be taxed. No levy shall be levied on sweetened beverages which are manufactured locally in retail outlets and are intended for immediate consumption. Similarly, sweetened drinks delivered outside Estonia are not taxed in economic and professional activities. The estimated tax revenue under the draft is EUR 15 million in 2018, EUR 16 million in 2019 and EUR 17 million in 2020. The draft is planned to enter into force on 1 January 2018. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U633	Thank you. I will start from a distance on your question of how we have managed to contain or limit it. I would say that border trade has exactly responded to what we have expected. We can talk about alcohol duty today because there is no excise duty on sweetened beverages yet. Moreover, in the spring, when the country's budget strategy was drawn up, macro-experts adjusted the receipts of alcohol excise duties to a higher level. They had rather been conservative. What do I mean by that? When we look at the receipts from excise goods in the state budget, we see that they have been obtained according to expert analysis. Border trade in the order of 5 to 6% is fully accounted for in the case of alcohol, and we can see that this is exactly what has been happening, rather a little less. As far as tax policy decisions in other countries are concerned, each country is, of course, sovereign in its tax policy decisions. No one can impose anything on anyone; other countries cannot interfere with national policies. By the way, since we can talk today about a change in excise duty, which has already entered into force, Lithuania has just raised excise duty on alcohol, beer excise duty at more than 100%. So I think it gives Latvia the opportunity to make changes to its excise policy, if necessary, if it is to be used. But this is, of course, the internal desire or task of each country itself. And if you are asking whether this price of sweetened drinks in the current example can in any way affect border trade, then surely a slight increase in border trade will be accompanied by this tax increase, whatever the case may be. But we don't see it as an increase that's going to cause a budget gap.

We don't just need tax evasion. And, all of that, the Free Party Group proposes to reject the draft law on the tax on sweetened beverages initiated by the Government of the Republic at first reading. ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U797	The next item is the first reading of the draft Act amending the Income Tax Act, the VAT Act and the Traffic Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. Minister of Finance Sven Sester, please.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U798	Hello, Mr President.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U799	Hi!
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U800	Dear Members, The night's still young. Let's move on to our next bill. This is a bill bearing the number 458. For all those who do not know what is behind this number, this is a draft law amending the Income Tax Act and other laws, mainly a draft law amending the VAT and traffic law. [[The manager's ringing the bell.]] With the draft submitted to you, we will change the rules on the taxation of participation options in the Income Tax Act. Two corporate tax measures included in the Government's Action Programme are implemented and the Estonian branches of resident credit institutions and non-resident credit institutions are obliged to pay advances on income tax. In addition, the draft amends the rules on the taxation of passenger cars used by the company in the VAT Act and the basis for calculating the price of the special benefit for passenger cars in the Income Tax Act. Under the current Income Tax Act, the disposal of participation options is exempt if the period between the provision and disposal of the participation option is three years. Where this period is shorter, the special preferential tax shall be the special benefit. The purpose of the Act is to encourage start-up participation options through a more favourable tax treatment. In the case of the sale of the company's total holding, i.e. "full exit', and in the case of loss or death of the employee's capacity, a three-year retention period is not required in the future. In the future, if the underlying assets of the option change, the three-year maintenance period will not be interrupted, provided that all other contractual terms remain unchanged. In order to support the business environment and improve the availability of labour, the tax regime for employers' costs related to accommodation and transport of workers will be changed. The cost of accommodation for an employee is tax-free for the employer if the employee resides more than 50 kilometres from the place of employment. The employer's transport costs shall not be treated as a special advantage if the transport is carried out by bus. The employer shall also be allowed to cover the transport costs of the employee working under the contract of employment if the worker's place of residence is at least 50 kilometres from the place of employment. In order to improve the competitiveness of the Estonian investment environment, the company's regular profit distributions will be subject to a lower income tax rate of 14%. This is necessary in order for Estonia to be able to compete with the 15% income tax rate of Lithuania and Latvia, for example, in attracting new investments. This amount, which is less than or equal to the average of the company's distributed profits that were subject to taxation in Estonia in the previous three years, is treated as a regular profit distribution. For the purposes of calculating the average distributed profit for the preceding three calendar years, the first year shall be considered as 2018. This means that in 2019 the 14% rate will be applied to one third of the profits distributed in 2018, etc. The share of profits taxed at a rate of 14% on dividends paid to a natural person is retained by the company for 7% of its income tax, the tax burden on a self-employed person or shareholder does not change as compared to the present draft. The draft solves the problem of non-taxing hidden profit distributions. According to the draft law, the distribution of profits is to be regarded as loans granted by a subsidiary to a parent company or to another company located above the chain of companies, which exceed the amount of the loans and capital contributions taken by the company. If the loan is repaid within two years of its granting, the State shall repay the tax paid to the taxpayer when the loan is granted. By way of derogation, it is stipulated that the income tax liability does not generate money in the short term through a group account to other group members if it is intended to manage the liquidity of the group as a whole. Thus, the normal business activities envisaged in the draft are not disrupted, but the Estonian tax environment is regulated. The bill imposes an obligation on resident credit institutions and Estonian branches of non-resident credit institutions to pay advances on income tax. The rate of advance payments is 14% of the profit earned in the previous quarter. A tax scheme based on advance payments for banks ensures a more regular collection of income tax into the state budget. In order to reduce the administrative burden on taxpayers, the Income Tax Act simplifies the rules for calculating tax-free income. The amendment does not affect the amount of tax-free income. Payments to volunteers who carry out NGO functions in the public interest in a foreign country, up to the limit of the daily allowance of an external mission, are not subject to income tax under the proposal. In the future, the employer may reimburse tax-free parking fees paid for the use of a personal passenger car for the performance of service, work and official tasks. The price of an employer's passenger car special benefit is determined on the basis of the capacity of the passenger car. The current maximum amount of EUR 256 per month per passenger car is replaced by a calculation of the capacity of the car, according to which the tax liability for the special benefit, i.e. income tax and social tax, is EUR 1.3 per kilowatt per month. Under the new system, the tax liability is lower than the fixed rate of the current special benefit for mid- and low-power cars. By the way, they account for about 70% of companies' passenger cars. For passenger cars older than five years, the tax liability is a quarter lower. In the case of passenger cars which have been banned for private use by the employer, including cars which are in the public sector, I draw attention to, in the public sector, only used for professional travel, the corresponding note shall be entered in the traffic register. In the case of mixed-use vans (N1), operators will be allowed to use on a voluntary basis the same KWTP calculation basis as for passenger cars. At present, they are subject to market-based taxation of their own resources and benefits to employees, which is difficult and burdensome in tax accounting. These are the main changes to the draft under discussion. Thank you for your attention.

And we agreed on this in everything, including these specific numbers, that 1.3 euros in kilowatts, etc. True, if there is no change in behaviour, this new, kilowatt-based calculation will be negative for the state budget, i.e. if there is no change in behaviour. We expect a behavioural change to come. If you ask why there will be a behavioural change, we assume that because 70% of current cars will be taxed with a smaller number. If, in the past, regardless of the value of the car itself, regardless of the kilowatts of the car, the price of the car was always 170 euros, then it is now based on kilowatts and this amount may be less than 100 euros, a hundred euros, there are examples for you. 70% of cars! We expect a behavioural change, which, in sum, should lead to a situation where this minus, which we are now creating through this behavioural change in the bill, becomes a plus. Once again, I think it will make it easier, eliminate the driving diaries, and ultimately, 70% of car owners will pay less in the future.

It is a stem problem, as the previous speaker said, that some things would like to be supported, but since everything is put in a single set, we cannot support them. As a result of the above, we propose to reject the draft at first reading. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U909	Today's 13th item is the first reading of the draft law amending the State Budget Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. I'd like to ask Sven Sester, Minister of Finance, as the initiator's waitress.
ParlaMint-EE_2017-05-17_U910	Hi! I'll give you an overview of the bill bearing number 456. For all those who do not know what is behind this number, this is a draft law amending the state budget law. Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! You really have before you a draft law amending the State Budget Act. The State Budget Act is the most important law that directs the financial management of the state. By this law, you members of the Riigikogu have established clear frameworks for organising state finances. There have been three impulses to change these frameworks: your own proposals, the goals of the government union and the need to reduce the workload of budgeting. First of all, we discussed the organisation of Estonian fiscal policy as an important national issue in this Chamber last 14 June. The starting point for the discussion was the report prepared by the Riigikogu, which also made proposals for legislative changes. Several of them will implement the bill before you. For example, when the draft is adopted as a law, the government is obliged to present an overview of the investments planned in the next four years with the state budget. The government's priority is economic growth. Only faster growth will enable more action to be taken in terms of population, security and people's well-being. In monetary policy, it means stimulating the economy when economic growth is below natural levels and cooling when the economy is starting to overheat. The draft clarifies the rules so that the structural surplus of previous years can be used in the future to stimulate the economy by means of investment. This opportunity will be used by the government in the coming years. I would like to stress once again that this does not mean abandoning the current fiscal policy, but that we look at the objective of balance as a multi-annual average. If there is no previous surplus, it will not be possible to use it, and so the budget will always be balanced in the long term. Nor does such an arrangement imply significant risks to the budget, because we are planning to make investments that are final and not to increase running costs. The most important asset in Estonia is people's time and it is important to use it wisely. Work that does not create value must be avoided in the public sector. Under the current law, we prepare and hold fiscal debates three times each year. The change in the state budget strategy, the state budget as well as the state budget for the current year need to be dealt with separately. All this, even if there has been no change in the economic situation and objectives. The draft will make the government's work in preparing the state budget more flexible. Honourable Riigikogu! Now I will be a little more specific about the major changes to the draft. I share these three groups: improvements to the budget information submitted to the Riigikogu, changes to the structural balance rule and changes in the preparation of budgetary documents. After last year's debate, we have been looking for ways to make the longer view of public finances more accessible and understandable to you and the public. I agree that budgetary policy in the Riigikogu must be shaped beyond one financial year. There is a bill before you that will give you more information to process the budget. From now on, you will get a longer view of public finances. The four-year view of the budgetary strategy is presented in the explanatory memorandum to the state budget. The explanatory memorandum to the national budget describes the impact of budgetary decisions not for one year but for four years. So far, the multi-annual view of fiscal policy has been the government's responsibility. The amendment of the State Budget Act will provide both the public and the Riigikogu with a better overview of the state's financial situation and plans in the next four years. In addition, the draft imposes an obligation on the government to provide a more comprehensive description of the planned economic activity. This year's state budget has been drawn up for the first time on an accrual basis. This means that the same financial forecasts used to work in the private sector can be included in the state budget. The explanatory memorandum to the state budget will provide you with the planned investments, the budgetary balance sheet and the forecast of the state's cash flows. In turn, the accrual basis is a prerequisite for the use of an activity-based budget. In the above mentioned report on the fiscal policy of the Riigikogu, you proposed to move to an activity-based budget as soon as possible. The State Budget Act established that the changeover had to be made in the 2020 budget. The activity-based budget provides clarity on how much the country will contribute to achieving the objectives set in the various areas. The objectives are mostly due to the development plans discussed in the Riigikogu and describe the activities and services that aim to achieve the objectives. The transition to an ABB in 2020 means that Estonia is one of the first countries to try to build a state budget with an overview of the state's costs per service provided. The addition of performance areas and programmes to the state budget changes the structure of the State Budget Act. The current share of the Government of the Republic in the annual budget has now been distributed among the ministries. An activity-based budget will focus on areas and programmes. The possibilities for cooperation between ministries in the use of the budget will be increased, giving the government the right to decide on the allocation of state budget resources between government areas. To this end, the annual state budget law approved by the Riigikogu excludes the distribution of funds to ministries, which is replaced by a division between fields and programmes. Good Riigikogu! I know that many of you are interested in the change to the structural balance rule in the draft budget. The amendment is necessary to maximise the budget's support for growth. We want to prevent the government from over-cooling the economy with its actions or, on the contrary, from heating up the boom. The structural balance of the public budget will be followed as a longer-term average in the future. In the future, the surplus of previous years may be used to make investments in an amount equal to up to 0.5% of GDP. In recent years, the government budget has been in surplus. With the build-up of structural surpluses, we have stimulated economic growth less than would have been economically sensible. In other words, we have been doing national budgets for several years, one of the monetary and economic policy objectives of which has been to stimulate the economy, but at the end of the financial year it has always become clear that revenue has been received better than planned or that expenditure has not been incurred. This has left some of the money planned undirected to the economy. Thus, we have rather cooled the economy with budgetary policy, and the current law has not allowed us to correct this mistake next year. With this bill, we will correct this mistake. The revision of the balancing rule allows investment to be made on account of the surplus of the previous year. This will allow us to make strategic investments for up to 117 million euros in 2018. These investments will increase Estonia's economic growth and improve its defence capabilities. With these changes, we will continue to pursue a balanced fiscal policy. If there was no surplus in previous years, a balanced budget must be drawn up. Thus, the average for the budgetary period is still in structural equilibrium. In the case of surpluses, forward-looking investments are allowed, but at the same time, if a deficit occurs, it will have to be covered by a surplus in the coming years. The new balance rule is in line with the European budget rules. The government's aim is always to maintain sufficient reserves for crises. The changes in the balancing rule concern the national budget for 2018 and subsequent years after entry into force. The rule as a whole excludes major differences in structural balance. If, however, they arise, I mean both over- and deficit, the changes will always guide fiscal policy towards achieving a balance. Good Riigikogu! Last but not least, I want to talk about the direction taken in the draft to reduce the internal bureaucracy of the government. The Estonian public sector must be effective. If work does not create significant added value, it must be abandoned. With the draft, we will combine the draft state budget for the following year with the preparation of the national budget strategy, i.e. the four-year plan for public finances. The main difficulty in drawing up the draft state budget in the government will remain in spring. The rules governing the procedure of the state budget in the Riigikogu do not change. In addition, other amendments have been made to the draft with the aim of ensuring sound working arrangements and legal clarity. For example, the National Audit Office's proposals provide clarification in the wording of the regulation on the management of the state's cash flows. The State's cash flow management is organised in the country as a whole by the Treasury Department of the Ministry of Finance. Clarifications will also be made in the use of funds from national support programmes and in the rules for monitoring the implementation of loans granted by the State and the state budget. In summing up, the changes to the State Budget Act are aimed at the future. If you adopt the draft as a law, the next Riigikogu, together with the draft State Budget Act 2020, will receive the plan for the next four years of public finances. The budget shall also identify the expenditure planned up to the cost of the operations. The budget balance rule will be updated so that the structural balance of the state budget will be followed as a longer-term average in the future. The surplus of previous years can be used for investments. In order to ensure a balanced fiscal policy in structural terms, the rule remains that if a deficit occurs, it must be compensated by a surplus. Estonia's fiscal and monetary policy will continue to be conservative and public finances in good order. Honourable Riigikogu, I ask you to support the draft. Thank you.

Now it is a question of what kind of forecast should be taken into account. Here the law states very clearly that the state budget is not made in the light of the OECD, IMF or Eesti Pank economic forecast, but in the light of the Ministry of Finance's economic forecast. That's what we have to say. You also mentioned the economics of whether or not the conjunction contributes. Yes, of course, Estonia, with its open dynamic economy, depends very strongly on our neighbours and export partners and their import capacity. This is a complex approach: on the one hand, their ability to import, i.e. our export capacity, and on the other hand, fiscal policy decisions taken in Estonia in order to increase our competitiveness, and budgetary decisions to boost our economy. It's a complex view. If you ask whether the well-being of our neighbours and their economic growth will contribute to our well-being, of course, but it is also the opposite: their more difficult situation automatically creates a reduction in our export capacity.

The budget balance was followed earlier in the preparation of the budgets, but this principle was not laid down in law. In the past, the principle of balance was contained in coalition agreements. According to the amendment, each spring, the structural balance of the previous year will be fixed and added to the earlier amount when the stability programme is drawn up.
